**This is the ONLY open-work list** (owner, 2026-09-12). Handoff memories, the
audit-bus ledger and agent reports are evidence, not backlogs: anything still
open from them gets a line here, with the check that guards it once one exists.

**Layout (reconciled 2026-09-23, Q16):** (1) the live QUEUE, verbatim, first;
(2) every item still open from older sections, one line each with its current
status and a pointer into the archive; (3) the 2026-09-02 ledger's leftovers
(docs/audit/OPEN_ITEMS.md was retired, then deleted 2026-09-27; history in docs/archive/OPEN_ITEMS-2026-09-02.md); (4) the audit bus, by
reference. History (closed sections, narratives) is verbatim in
[docs/archive/OPEN-history-2026-09.md](archive/OPEN-history-2026-09.md),
with a reconcile log of every item closed and its evidence. Nothing was deleted.

**Done items leave this file (Q16, 2026-09-26):** a ticked `- [x]` item is moved
verbatim to the dated archive docs/archive/OPEN-done-YYYY-MM.md by
`npm run inventories:refresh` (scripts/archive-done.mjs); check:generated fails
while one is left here. The queue score, next free number and the done-item
guard check read this file AND the archives, so archiving never changes a count.

**`[~]` items say when they are done (2026-09-27):** put what is left as one or
more markers on the item, written out in full and ANDed together:
``done-when: sql `SELECT <one column>` => <expected>`` (read-only, first row),
`done-when: test <path>`, `done-when: issue #N closed`, or `done-when: pr #N merged`.
Only add markers when they cover everything that is left (a screenshot or a
review still to do means no marker yet). `node scripts/open-done-when.mjs` runs them;
the nightly `open-done-when` workflow opens one nightly-red issue listing the items
that are ready to tick. src/test/openPartlyDoneItemsSayDoneWhen.test.ts ratchets the
count of `[~]` items with no marker.

**Priority among the open queue items (lead's order, 2026-09-23 — money and
security first, then alerting, then the audit, then hygiene):** Q53 (DB outage
root cause), Q50 (card holds on cancelled jobs, Stripe side), Q3 (Stripe test
balance + monitor), Q30 (missed daily crons never catch up), Q2 (test runs raise
real alerts), Q42 (open alert-ledger items), Q9 (skipped money/authz review),
Q14 (Supabase security advisors), Q45 (prove a restore), Q58 (one place
for everything open), Q52 (false-green hunt), Q54 (front/back parity), Q35 (exhaustive
audit), Q44 (partial gates turn main red), Q11/Q15/Q39 (Sentry + error
screens), Q10 (owner-side), then the rest in number order. An item's own line
is the source of truth for its state; this sentence only orders them.

- [ ] **Q7 MEDIUM WebKit only: the bottom nav isn't frosted. OWNER (device), re-checked 2026-09-26: still unmeasured in WebKit.** Nothing code-side can settle it. The dock is signed-in only (guest /browse renders no MobileNav, checked at 375), the cloud session that took this item had no test-account credentials, and Playwright WebKit on Linux is not the iOS compositor, so no CI run can show WKWebView frost either. What the source does (MobileNav.tsx:705-745): the full-width curtain carries `backdropFilter`/`WebkitBackdropFilter: blur(32px) saturate(170%)` together with `maskImage`/`WebkitMaskImage` on the SAME element, and the pill carries `blur(40px) saturate(180%)` inside the nav, whose own style has `transform` + `willChange: transform` (the hide-on-scroll slide). Both are inline styles, so the CSS minifier cannot have dropped the -webkit- form. **Ask:** on the iOS 26.1 simulator or a device, signed in, open /home, scroll the feed under the dock and send one screenshot. **Recommendation:** if the pill is frosted and only the band above it is clear, the curtain's mask is the suspect (test by removing `WebkitMaskImage` alone). If the pill is clear too, test with `willChange` removed from the nav. Fix whichever one screenshot proves, and add a pixel-variance check under the dock to a WebKit spec. (Q1101 merged here 2026-10-03, verify pass: the same work: "LOW WebKit only: bottom nav is not frosted (feed text shows sharp under icons) — Not reached — needs device/s")

5. **Push notifications (Q82): three things only you can do.** The code
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-26 (owner pop-up): "cut it when main is current".** Claude runs fastlane beta from main
   once the open landings are in and main CI is green; you install it and open the app once.
   cause is fixed and pushed (see Q82), but no phone has the fix yet.
   (a) **Cut a TestFlight build from main** (at or after the Q82 fix commit)
   and install it on your iPhone. The only builds with the AppDelegate
   forwarding are ce4d38d59 (2026-09-02) and later, and every build so far
   also has the boot race (it depends on timing, so it may not hit every
   launch). (b) **On that build: sign in, go to Home, tap "Enable" on the
   notifications pill, then Allow.** The pill only shows after you've posted
   or applied, or on a later launch more than an hour after install. If you tapped "Don't
   Allow" on any earlier build, iOS will not ask again: turn it on in
   Settings > Helpr > Notifications first, then cold-launch the app. Within
   seconds `select count(*) from push_tokens` should be 1 and
   analytics_events should show `push_token_saved`. If it doesn't, the
   analytics events say which step failed. (c) **Only if you run the app
   straight from Xcode on your phone:** those builds get SANDBOX APNs tokens,
   and prod sends to the production APNs host (`APNS_USE_SANDBOX` is unset),
   so APNs rejects them with BadDeviceToken and the sender deletes the row.
   TestFlight/App Store builds are unaffected. Leave this alone unless you
   test from Xcode. APNs credentials are fine: APNS_KEY_ID, APNS_TEAM_ID
   (digest matches P85MCK558V), APNS_BUNDLE_ID (digest matches com.Helpr)
   and APNS_AUTH_KEY are all set in Supabase secrets. Whether the .p8 key
   itself is valid can't be checked without a real token to send to.

7. **Turn off Supabase's GitHub auto-deploy to production (Q121). Top priority.**
   On every push to main, Supabase's GitHub integration applies new migrations
   (and deploys edge functions) to prod within about a minute. Our deploy
   workflow's safety checks (lint, replay test, destructive-DDL stop) run after
   that, so for the last 24h (24 migrations) they have not blocked anything.
   Measured from prod's own logs 2026-09-23 (Q117). What you do: Supabase
   dashboard > Project Settings > Integrations > GitHub, and switch off
   automatic deploys to the production branch (exact toggle name not verified).
   Until then, each new migration turns db-deploy red by design (the new
   provenance check), so the side channel stays visible.
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-26 (owner asked "whats the best option?"): switch it OFF.** Measured: db-deploy
   36261725456 (18:16Z) judged 124 prod versions since the cutoff, 108 with a db-deploy receipt and 16
   acknowledged side-channel applies, the newest of them 2026-09-23 ~16:10Z (Q194). So nothing has come
   in by the side channel for about 3 days, but that is not proof the toggle is off. Off is the only
   setting where db-deploy's lint, replay and destructive-DDL checks actually run before prod changes.
   Still the owner's dashboard click; the provenance check stays as the detector either way.

7. **Backups (Q45): the restore is proven weekly now; five things are yours.**
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-23 (owner pop-ups): (a) yes, Pro is paid and intended; (b) keep 14-day retention; (c) back up uploaded files EXCEPT id-documents, encrypted (work item Q147); (d) no PITR (no paid upgrade); (e) BACKUP_PASSPHRASE and ban_fingerprint_salt go in the owner's password manager (owner to-do).**
   Measured 2026-09-23. Nothing below is needed for the drill to stay green.
   (a) **Is the Supabase org really on Pro?** The API says `plan: "pro"`, and
   `supabase backups list` shows 7 daily platform backups. Every doc said
   "free tier". If you are paying $25/mo, you already have in-place daily
   restores for 7 days. If you did not mean to be on Pro, that is a bill.
   (b) **Backup retention is 14 days, not 90.** The repo caps artifacts and
   logs at 14 days (`maximum_allowed_days` 90). Raising it to 90 is one
   setting, but it lengthens EVERY workflow's artifacts, and on the free
   GitHub plan that can go past the artifact-storage quota (billed). The
   alternative is a monthly copy somewhere else. Your call.
   (c) **Storage files (photos, ID documents) are in NO backup**: 152 objects,
   about 10 MB (proof-photos 64, job-photos 61, message-attachments 18,
   user-documents 4, avatars 4, id-documents 1). The platform backup excludes
   them too. Backing them up needs a service-role key as an Actions secret and
   puts ID documents (encrypted) into a GitHub artifact: a privacy trade-off.
   (d) **PITR** (restore to the minute) is a paid add-on, about $100/mo plus
   Small compute. Without it, worst-case loss is about 24 h (platform) or
   24 h + GitHub's up-to-7 h schedule delay (ours). You said no paid upgrades,
   so this is only the measured gap.
   (e) **Two secrets that no backup holds.** `BACKUP_PASSPHRASE`: is there a
   copy outside GitHub? If not, losing the GitHub account also loses every
   backup. `ban_fingerprint_salt` (vault): it cannot be re-created, and
   without it every stored ban fingerprint stops matching after a restore.
   Want it (encrypted) in the backup, or kept in your password manager?

15. **Tax forms (W-9) after a Helpr deletes their account (CS-003, 2026-09-24).**
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-24 (owner pop-up): keep 4 years after signing, then delete automatically. Work item Q370.**
   Deleting an account leaves the Helpr's W-9 record (typed legal name, signing IP)
   in place forever. The IRS expects a business to keep W-9s for about 4 years, so
   deleting them at once may be wrong too. Options: (a) keep them 4 years after
   deletion, then delete automatically; (b) delete them with the account; (c) keep
   forever. I recommend (a), but it is a legal call. No W-9s exist in prod yet.

   then delete them automatically; (b) delete them as soon as the account is
   deleted. I recommend (a).

    crew completes when every hired member is done, and the unfilled shares go back to the poster.

- [ ] **Q1057** MEDIUM OWNER: STRIPE_TEST_SECRET_KEY secret; reconnect Supabase/Slack/Canva connectors — OWNER: STRIPE_TEST_SECRET_KEY secret; reconnect Supabase/Slack/Canva connectors (see also 5692) (archive L5749)
- [ ] **Q1058** MEDIUM Alerts to one Slack channel: prod down, deploy failed, nightly-red, Stripe webhook failures, DB near — Alerts to one Slack channel: prod down, deploy failed, nightly-red, Stripe webhook failures, DB near free-tier limits — PARTLY DONE (archive L5755) **RE-MEASURED 2026-10-05 (alerts lane):** prod down (uptime.yml), deploy failed (db-deploy, functions-deploy, deploy), Stripe webhook failures (stripe-webhook via _shared/slack-alerts.ts) and DB limits (supabase-usage.yml) all post to SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL. The one class that never reaches Slack is a NEW nightly-red issue: nightly-issue-sync only opens/comments, the ledger sync records it, and the daily Slack digest (cron ops-daily-digest -> send_ops_daily_digest()) summarises error_logs only (0 nightly-related error_logs rows in the last 3 days, read-only SQL). Tried and reverted: posting from the composite action breaks three guards by design (opsAlertLedgerCoverage: a Slack-posting step must record the ledger in that step; slackAlertWorkflows: such a step must warn when empty; workflowFalseGreenShapes: no `|| echo`). Two ways that fit them: (a) add the open ledger items (nightly_red included) to send_ops_daily_digest() (a migration; daily, not instant), or (b) have `ops-alert-ledger.mjs sync` call the slack-ops-alert transport when it records a FIRST occurrence of a nightly_red item (instant at the next prod-errors run, which GitHub throttles to hours apart). Lead to pick; then build with a guard.

(Q165 carry-over, 2026-09-23.)** Evidence: src/hooks/useMapKitJs.ts:295-300; supabase/functions/mapkit-token/index.ts:9,27-33. Needs live check: POST /functions/v1/mapkit-token returns 503 not_configured or 200. Owner sets APPLE_MAPKIT_PRIVATE_KEY/_KEY_ID/_TEAM_ID, then drop the fallback. Q75 covers only the re-mint. From: docs/archive/VISUAL-2026-08-24.md + docs/archive/FABLE-LEAD-2026-08-23.md. **STATUS 2026-10-05 (security-data lane, measured live with curl POST /functions/v1/mapkit-token):** the origin-locked path IS live: HTTP 200 with a 1-hour ES256 token (kid 4QA8J9TA8K, iss P85MCK558V) whose origin claim is the caller's Origin (https://www.louisianahelpr.com -> that origin), so the owner's secrets are set; the build-time fallback can now be removed (the owner's order was secrets first, then remove). NEW FINDING, same function: the origin claim is copied from the caller-controlled Origin header with no allowlist (Origin: https://evil.example.com -> a token locked to evil.example.com), and an Origin of capacitor://localhost (or none) mints a token with NO origin claim, so anyone can mint a usable unrestricted token (rate-limited only); origin-locking therefore protects nothing yet. LEFT (not built here; touches useMapKitJs, its tests, the expiry monitor and 4 workflows): (a) allowlist web origins (louisianahelpr.com, www, the dev/preview hosts) and refuse others with 403; decide what the native app gets (the WebView cannot carry an origin, so an unrestricted token there needs another gate, e.g. a signed app-attest or accept the risk); (b) remove VITE_APPLE_MAPKIT_TOKEN and its fallback. GUARD when built: a live check that a foreign Origin is refused and the bundle carries no MapKit token.

Three layers, from the logs of runs 5-9 (36055191338, 36069105982, 36158775025, 36165226511, 36186004139): (1) request budget over the 400/min ceiling on main (752 and 733/min, runs 7 and 9): FIXED by porting ed86c0647 from PR #1797 (paceTo + meteredRunsArePaced guard); (2) real skeleton breaches on /jobs, /posts, /user/:id: NOT carried here; #1797 owns them (its 43e716d59 was ported, then reverted because e2e/prod-audit/activity-loading-reserve.spec.ts `jobs` pitch is red before mutation on it, vacuity run 36170345228), so they stay baselined debt under Q201 until #1797 lands; (3) THE MEASUREMENT DID NOT REPEAT: runs 7 and 9 on the SAME commit 3569359 disagreed on 21 of 139 surface lines (78 vs 73 measured; `helper /profile` cl=2/2 vs no-placeholder), so the two-way baseline churned (stale entries differ run to run). ROOT CAUSE: one browser context per persona was reused across every surface while the app persists React Query to IndexedDB (src/lib/queryPersister.ts), so a surface visited after a sibling that fetched the same queries rehydrated and drew no skeleton (or a subset), in an order set by two racing workers. FIXED: measure-loading-states.mjs opens a fresh context per surface and closes it. GUARD: src/test/loadingStatesColdContext.test.ts (red on the pre-fix file; 4 @mutate each red; fixture shows per-persona and per-viewport sharing rejected). Also: check-staleness.mjs counted a green run on ANY branch (`gh run list` had no --branch) and a branch dispatch's `land`/`notify` would land a branch measurement and open/close main's nightly-red issue; now WORKFLOW_BOUND entries carry `branch: "main"` (guard: stalenessWatch.test.ts, 2 new @mutate red) and land/notify run on refs/heads/main only. CONFIRMED CAUSE (2026-09-26): cold runs 36201048187 and 36202873061 measured 38 and 41 of 139 with DIFFERENT sets, and every miss read 'no loading placeholder at any settled stage (0 wave(s) released)': nextStage returned 'empty' on the blank BOOT frame, in the gap between the last chunk landing and the app's first query (screen unchanged 600ms, nothing held, nothing in flight). Reproduced locally on anon /browse at 6x CPU throttle: old logic 0/6 measured, fixed logic 6/6 (unthrottled 3/3; /login, /help, 404 still end 'empty'). FIX: 'empty' needs the app booted (visible text in #root; the route fallback's sr-only 'Loading…' does not count) and the WIRE quiet as well as the screen. - [~] **Q335 MEDIUM Deleted-account threads merge per job and can never be hidden; thread_pins/mutes/archives keep rows for deleted users (Q262, 2026-09-24).** Owner decision 2026-09-26: "let people archive deleted-account threads" (merge per job kept; no pin or mute). Done on branch cloud/open-messaging-deleted: migration 20260926041106_thread_archives_deleted_party makes thread_archives.other_user_id nullable (NULL = the job's deleted-account thread) under a surrogate `id` PRIMARY KEY + UNIQUE NULLS NOT DISTINCT (user_id, job_id, other_user_id), so one such row per viewer per job and the client's upsert(onConflict) still lands on it; other_user_id keeps ON DELETE CASCADE (SET NULL would collide with an existing NULL row and fail account deletion); RLS owner-only unchanged. Client: archivedConversations keys the null party as `deleted-account` and restores it with `.is("other_user_id", null)` (23502 before deploy keeps the local mirror); swipe-to-archive (left only, no pin trail), multi-select, Recently Deleted and Restore now take it; the single-hide dialog no longer promises "it'll come back if they send you a new message" for a deleted account. lh-authz-rls REVIEW-ONLY (2026-09-26): migration, RLS and cascade correct (re-run on a real PG16 server and PGlite 18; PG15 itself not run); two merge-up defects fixed in the same PR: a pre-Q335 build's `${job}_null` mirror key (22P02) and an archive of someone who later deleted (23503) each failed the whole batch on every load; keys are now canonicalised/validated and a failed batch retries row by row, dropping rows the server can never take (3 more @mutate lines in messagesReceiverNullable, each shown red). Guards: messagesReceiverNullable "a deleted-account thread can be archived, never pinned (Q335)" (6 @mutate lines, each shown red, incl. a behaviour case on the archive mirror) + src/test/threadPartyForeignKeys.test.ts (every other_user_id column CASCADEs; 2 @mutate lines shown red). The "no FK" half of the original report was not true of the migrations: all three tables were created with other_user_id REFERENCES auth.users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE. NOT verified live (no prod credentials in the session). Left: after db-deploy, `SELECT is_nullable FROM information_schema.columns WHERE table_schema='public' AND table_name='thread_archives' AND column_name='other_user_id'` = YES and `SELECT pg_get_constraintdef(oid) FROM pg_constraint WHERE conrelid='public.thread_archives'::regclass` lists PRIMARY KEY (id) + UNIQUE NULLS NOT DISTINCT; the FK query in threadPartyForeignKeys.test.ts returns 3 rows with 'c'; then on a test account, swipe a "Former member" thread to archive, find it in Recently Deleted, restore it, and screenshot at 375 and 1440. **SQL half VERIFIED LIVE 2026-09-27 (read-only execute_sql): both migrations 20260926015040 + 20260926041106 in schema_migrations; thread_archives.other_user_id is_nullable=YES; constraints thread_archives_pkey PRIMARY KEY (id) + thread_archives_user_job_other_key UNIQUE NULLS NOT DISTINCT (user_id, job_id, other_user_id); other_user_id FK confdeltype 'c' on thread_archives, thread_mutes, thread_pins (3 rows). FIX: on 'won', when no clawback was repaid and the ONE thing holding a 'chargeback' job is a decided single-Helpr split that has not run, the payment state the decision left is restored (preDecisionPaymentStatus: payout_pending only with a schedule, else escrow), a compare-and-set on status + payment_status + the dispute_status read, KEEPING disputed_at, so an admin can Retry settlement and no automatic payout can; ANY other hold (an open dispute or a 'disputed' job, a crew decision, a legacy group decision, a settlement claim, a reversed payout) keeps the job 'chargeback' and pages CRITICAL 'settle it by hand' with the manual step (no admin tool can settle a 'chargeback' job) (each has an automatic settler the restore would switch back on: lh-money-escrow review HIGH). GUARD: src/test/decidedDisputeReachesTerminal.test.ts (won = restores, gated on decidedSplitOnly) and src/test/edge/disputeSettlementWebhook.test.ts (Q449 block, 12 cases, red on main's handler); every @mutate killed by hand. LEFT: confirm in Stripe TEST mode that a charge whose card dispute was WON can still be refunded (the split's refund leg runs on exactly that charge, after its transfer leg); do it in the same test-mode run as Q805 (1). Until then the admin's 'Chargeback WON' notice says to check that in Stripe before Retry settlement. Was: chargeDisputeClosed 'won' leaves the job payment_status='chargeback' unless a clawback was repaid; execute-dispute-split refuses 'chargeback', so the decided split cannot run and admins are only told "job still on hold". Needs: restore the pre-chargeback payment state on 'won' when an unexecuted decided dispute holds the job (as warning_closed does), after confirming in Stripe test mode that a charge whose dispute was won can still be refunded by the split. - [ ] **Q374 HIGH** (LAUNCH CHECKLIST, owner MQ21/24 2026-09-24, "you decide"): before launch, the owner's CPA answers (1) whether to add a Louisiana registration in Stripe Tax, (2) which job categories are taxable, and (3) whether the service fee is taxable. No code change until then. OWNER 2026-09-27: there is NO CPA; owner: "whatever you found online will work". The lead researches Louisiana law from public sources (not professional tax advice) and decides (1)-(3). LEAD DECIDED 2026-09-27 (from public sources: R.S. 47:301.3, 47:340.1, the LDR reform FAQ, RSIB 23-001; this is not professional tax advice): (1) KEEP the Louisiana registration in Stripe Tax. Check that live taxreg_1TAZVsKp2H4b7tECyvJINQyg still covers state and local tax. ADD the same registration in TEST mode (the likely cause of Q441's $0), re-run Q441, and record the tax in cents. Ask LDR or the Remote Sellers Commission whether LH files as a marketplace facilitator (one return) or with LDR plus each parish, before the first taxable sale. (2) Taxable categories: assembly (fabrication or repair of tangible personal property) and handyman stay taxable. Handyman over-collects on real-property work, which is the safer direction. All other categories stay exempt. Follow-up build: add a 'work on a movable item?' flag so handyman on real property is exempt and carpet, rug and upholstery cleaning is taxed. Also fix the salesTax.ts header ('ten services' should be 11). (3) Service fee: CONSERVATIVE. On a TAXABLE job, the fee takes the labor line's tax code (create-payment ~640-650, plus the recurring precompute ~1446-1469). It stays nontaxable on exempt jobs. Tips stay nontaxable. This is a money-path build, reviewed with lh-money-escrow and stripeTaxCodes.test.ts updated.

- [~] **Q380 MEDIUM** (alert, nightly-red #1719 e2e-journeys, 2026-09-24): 02-marketplace hire-and-message fails at 1440 on the "slow" network profile. The poster presses Message Helpr, which goes to `/messages?jobId=<job>&userId=<helper>` (confirmed in the trace); openConvo fetched the thread's messages, but the page ended on the list at /messages?chat=1 with no reply box. Reads as a race that closes the placeholder thread when the network is slow. The same deep link opens the thread with a reply box for helpr-e2e-poster-0902 on prod at normal speed (checked 2026-09-24). Evidence: the run's artifacts and trace, copied to the session scratchpad. ROOT CAUSE (trace): not a race. The inbox load, a full confirming refetch and then the placeholder lookup ran in series; a just-hired thread has no messages, so the refetch can never return it, and the placeholder's jobs read was still pending at the 30s timeout. FIXED 2026-09-24: the placeholder is built alongside the refetch (src/pages/messages/useMessagesData.ts). - [ ] **Q390** (MEDIUM, OA-004, 2026-09-25): OWNER QUESTION (2026-09-26): move the native session mirror from @capacitor/preferences (NSUserDefaults plist, in device backups) to the iOS Keychain / Android Keystore (kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly)? Recommendation: yes, before launch, in a native release; it needs a new plugin in the .ipa/.apk, so it ships only with a build, and a real-device check (relaunch, Offload App, WebKit localStorage eviction) that cannot run in a cloud session. Nothing code-side was changed here: switching the web code to a plugin that installed builds do not carry would log those users out. Original text: the native Supabase session (access + refresh token) is stored in NSUserDefaults via @capacitor/preferences (an unencrypted plist, in device backups, not passcode-gated) and in WKWebView localStorage. The misleading `keychainStorageAdapter` name is fixed (now `preferencesStorageAdapter`; guard `src/test/secureStorageNamesAreTrue.test.ts`). Open: owner decision whether to move the native mirror to a Keychain-backed plugin (kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly); needs a device check that sessions still survive relaunch, Offload App and WebKit localStorage eviction. No guard yet for the storage choice itself.

- [~] **Q511 MEDIUM** (lh-authz-rls review of Q335, 2026-09-26; pre-existing, not introduced by Q335): a Restore on one device was undone by another — unarchiveConversation deleted the server row, but a second device's local mirror still held the key and its next loadArchives merge-up pushed it back, hiding the thread everywhere. Fixed 2026-09-26 (owner: "fix Q511 too", branch cloud/open-messaging-deleted): archivedConversations keeps a per-user PENDING set (safeStorage `helpr_archived_conversations_pending_<uid>`) of archives this device made and never saw confirmed; archiveConversation marks pending and clears on a confirmed write (or rollback), unarchiveConversation clears it, and the merge-up (pure `planMergeUp`) pushes only pending keys and DROPS a local-only key the server confirmed once and no longer has. Upgrade note: a device's first load after this ships has no pending set, so any archive it made offline and never synced is dropped once (the thread reappears and can be archived again) — chosen over re-pushing, which is the bug. Guard: src/test/mirrorMergeUpPushesOnlyPending.test.ts (class = every src/lib module with a safeStorage mirror and an .upsert merge-up, derived from source; KNOWN_PUSHES_ALL exact both ways; 4 @mutate lines, each shown red). AVATAR CLASS (2026-09-27): the run's top repeated GET, `/storage/v1/object/public/avatars/:id/avatar.jpg` x719, was an APP defect, not the meter. UserAvatar set `src` before `crossOrigin` (React applies props in order), so WebKit/WKWebView started a no-cors load, then a second CORS load, and shared neither with the next <img> of the same URL (cache headers are fine: GET max-age=3600). Measured in Playwright WebKit, 40 <img> of one URL: src-first 41 requests, crossOrigin-first 1 (Chromium 1 either way). On a local build against prod /posts?filter=done, one Helpr avatar went 101 -> 1 request, all storage requests 95 -> 42 (evidence ~/.lh-shots/avatar-meter/). FIX: src/components/UserAvatar.tsx sets crossOrigin/loading before src. - [ ] **Q750 HIGH Q210(b) deferred money edges (lh-money-escrow review, 2026-09-27).** (1) A paid visit that later falls into a skip branch stays held until the visit-date sweep refunds it; (2) refunding a future-dated visit re-parks it; (3) Stripe's answer to a retried refund after "already refunded" is unverified; (4) end_recurring_series / parent job cancel do not expire the open Checkout (a late payment is refunded by webhook or sweep); (5) a Stripe session created but not stored is orphaned. Done when each is fixed or recorded as accepted, with a test. PROGRESS 2026-10-03 (edge tests red on main: 10 failed, all three files green after): (3) FIXED: Stripe documents `charge_already_refunded` for a refund of a charge with nothing left (docs.stripe.com/error-codes, read 2026-10-03; a key replays only within 24h); on main the webhook threw (critical "refund failed" page + 500, so Stripe redelivered) and the cron's createRefundOnce did the same. Both now count it, like an idempotency conflict, as done only when the intent carries a live refund (stripe-webhook.test.ts + charge-recurring-visits.test.ts "Q750 (3)"). (4) FIXED: on main a payment on a Checkout left open after the series ended or its parent was cancelled was marked PAID, held, and returned on the visit date LESS the card fee; the webhook now reads the parent and refunds it in full and expires the row (500 on an unreadable parent), and the daily sweep expires pending rows of an over series and closes their Checkout (stripe-webhook.test.ts / charge-recurring-visits.test.ts "Q750 (4)"). (5) FIXED: a session whose row stopped waiting before it was stored (zero rows) is expired at once; on a DB error it is left open on purpose so the same-key retry stores it (create-payment.test.ts "Q750 (5)"). (1) FIXED for a series that is OVER (ended or parent cancelled): the sweep reads the parent of every open future row (paged to the end), those parents' end state, then only the over parents' open rows (so its 500-row cap counts only those, lh-money-escrow review 2026-10-03), and refunds a paid one now under the Q808 cause rules; a paid row already booked on its intent is marked funded, never refunded ("Q750 (1)" and "Q750 (1) review" tests, plus a control that a live, disputed or unread series' rows are never read). Review follow-ups filed as Q1221-Q1224. STILL OPEN, OWNER DECISION: (1) for a PAUSED series (a ban or block on the Helpr, a date nobody holds, a dispute, a chargeback stop) the paid visit stays held until its date by design because it may still be booked; recommendation: keep holding it (≤ FUND_LEAD_DAYS = 3 days), since refunding early is what (2) turns into a second payment. (2) a series that is over never re-parks (asserted in "Q750 (1)"); a still-live, still-held date whose paid visit was refunded in the loop (insert refused, or the date changed hands mid-run, fee withheld per Q808) IS re-parked and the payer asked to pay again, the on-session twin of the off-session re-charge on a new hold id (20260927012806). Owner: accept that (recommended), or book the refused visit on the paid intent with the new holder instead of refunding. **OWNER DECISIONS (owner 2026-10-05 pop-up):** (1) KEEP HOLDING a paid visit's money while the series is paused (it resumes: the visit happens; the series ends: refund). (2) CHARGE FRESH: a refunded date that is booked again gets a new charge; never reuse the refunded intent. Both match today's code as described above; what is left is to pin each with a test that fails without it (not built; building paused 2026-10-05).

- [ ] **Q1170 MEDIUM A returning visitor's token refresh runs in series before every read on the first screen (found by the Q1158 lab work, 2026-10-03).** supabase-js refreshes an expired access token only once the app bundle has downloaded, and every read waits for it. Measured (local build, prod data, poster, cold, Chromium, scripts/perf/cwv-lab.mjs --expired, 375 Slow 4G + 4x CPU): /home `auth/v1/token?grant_type=refresh_token` 4485-5310 ms, first read at 5357, LCP 6396 -> 6996 ms (+600); /jobs refresh 3899-4876, LCP 4924 -> 5672 (+750); at 1440 +~200 ms. A real returning visitor's token is always expired after an hour away, so this is the common real-user load, not a corner. Fix to design: start the refresh from src/entry.ts beside the JS download and hand the result to supabase-js through an async storage getItem (never a second refresh with an already-used token); web only, native keeps its Preferences storage. AUTH change: needs an lh-authz-rls review before it lands. Done when the --expired lab run matches the fresh-token one, with a budget for it in e2e/web-vitals/first-screens.spec.ts.

The guest /browse already starts its job list from the entry beside the JS (src/boot/guestJobsPrefetch.ts, Q206: data-start 3796 -> 589 ms); the signed-in screens (profile row + the page's primary read) have no such path. Fix: the same pattern for the signed-in first screens, URL held equal to supabase-js's by a test (as src/test/guestJobsPrefetch.test.ts does). Done when the lab LCP of /home and /jobs at 375 drops by the data wait and the KNOWN ceilings in e2e/web-vitals/first-screens.spec.ts are lowered.
- [~] **Q1172 MEDIUM framer-motion rides two first paints: the dock arrives ~1 s after the page, and /home carries it before its first draw (found by the Q1158 lab work, 2026-10-03).** (1) MobileNav statically imports NavQuickMenu and SharedLayoutPill (framer-motion), so the lazy dock chunk waits on proxy.js (35 KB brotli): filmstrip of a cold /home at 375, Slow 4G + 4x CPU (~/.lh-shots/perf/film1-home-strip.png): title bar and skeleton at 4.4 s, dock at 5.4 s. (2) /home's route closure carries framer through BrowseTasksFeed -> SwipeableJobCard: proxy.js + animate.js (~40 KB brotli) download before its first draw (cwv-lab jsBeforeLcp). Fix: load framer on demand (the useFramerMotion pattern NotificationPanel uses, or LazyMotion), then start the dock's chunk with the page so it draws in the first frame; extend scripts/check-deferred-vendors.mjs from the boot graph to every first screen's route closure, red on today's /home first. Same paint-timing class as Q1158's page fades: OptimizedImage's `fadeIn` starts at opacity 0, which Chrome's LCP does not count, for job photos once the marketplace has them (not measurable on an empty marketplace). **FIXED IN PART 2026-10-04 (cloud/perf-first-paint, DRAFT PR; protection pending the lead's real-browser filmstrip and cwv-lab run).** (1) The dock: MobileNav no longer imports NavQuickMenu or SharedLayoutPill; DockPill draws the identical plain `<span>` (same element, classes, inline background) until src/components/mobileNav/dockMotion.ts has loaded, which useDockMotion starts once the page has settled (or at once on a long press, so the quick menu still opens), and never while the dock is hidden (guests, marketing pages). NavQuickMenuItem moved to its own file. (2) /home: SwipeableJobCard's framer-motion gesture is its own chunk (SwipeMotionLayer), loaded on page settle or the first touch of a card; until then the card is its content in a plain `relative z-10` div, and the content is portaled into one DOM node React never recreates, so the swap moves that node instead of remounting JobCard (no image-fade restart, no lost state; SwipeableJobCard.test.tsx asserts the same DOM node and state across the swap). Shared mechanism: src/lib/lazyModule.ts. Measured on the built bundle (npm run build, origin/main c68ac5f vs this branch; static closure of the route chunk, gzip): /home Dashboard 404 -> 360 KB (framer chunks proxy 39.1 + animate 4.7 + use-transform 0.7 + use-reduced-motion 0.2 KB gone), dock MobileNav 330 -> 289 KB (proxy 39.1 + AnimatePresence 2.0 KB gone), framer reachable from neither. GUARDS: scripts/check-deferred-vendors.mjs now walks the static closure of every ENTRY_ROUTE_CHUNKS route (read from src/boot/routePreload.ts, never copied) plus MobileNav and fails on a deferred package in any of them, with an exact two-way KNOWN_ROUTE_CLOSURE_VIOLATIONS (Messages, PostJob: Q1299); RED on the origin/main build (6 violations: Dashboard x4, MobileNav x2, plus the Q654 one), green after; vacuity: dropping a KNOWN entry, a stale KNOWN entry, a renamed ENTRY_ROUTE_CHUNKS, a static framer import planted in MobileNav and in SwipeableJobCard (rebuilt) are each red. Source half in vitest: src/test/deferredMotionImports.test.ts (@mutate x2, both red), DockPill.test.tsx, SwipeableJobCard.test.tsx, lazyModule.test.ts. NOT measured here: the dock-vs-page filmstrip and the 375 Slow-4G LCP/FCP (the lead runs those); the dock's pill no longer slides on a tab switch made before framer has loaded (after page settle, ~1 s), by design. Still open on this item: OptimizedImage's `fadeIn` starting at opacity 0 for job photos (not measurable on an empty marketplace).

- [ ] **Q1186 MEDIUM A pending accept completes only when something asks Stripe for the Helpr's status (lh-authz-rls review of Q1180, #4).** Prod's webhook receives no Connect events (0 account.* rows in stripe_webhook_events since the oldest retained row, 2026-09-04; Q876), so stripe_payouts_enabled only reaches true through stripe-connect `status`, which the app calls when the Helpr opens Profile or Activity. A Helpr who finishes Stripe and does not come back before the offer's deadline loses the offer (no strike). - [~] **Q1242 MEDIUM A person cannot delete a message the other party replied to (found while fixing Q1166, 2026-10-03; pre-existing; reproduced in PGlite on prod's bodies, not on prod).** messages_reply_to_id_fkey is ON DELETE SET NULL, and that cascade UPDATEs the reply while the deleter's request is still in place (auth.uid() = the deleter), so the BEFORE UPDATE triggers treat it as a client write: enforce_message_non_sender_read_only sees a non-sender changing reply_to_id and raises "a message may only be edited by the person who sent it", which fails the whole DELETE (the client shows "Couldn't delete that one"). Deleting a message nobody replied to, or one only you replied to, works. Live 2026-10-03: 1 reply, cross-party, so 1 message its sender cannot delete today; 0 error_logs rows with that text. Same result before and after 20261004001242 in PGlite. Fix direction: let the trigger accept reply_to_id going to NULL when the parent it named no longer exists (the FK's own cascade, which reaches it at pg_trigger_depth() > 1), never a client's own PATCH of reply_to_id; needs an lh-authz-rls review. GUARD when built: a PGlite probe (the sender deletes a message the receiver replied to: 1 row; the reply survives with reply_to_id NULL; a client still cannot clear reply_to_id on someone else's message). **FIXED 2026-10-05 (security-data lane, branch lane/security-data-1005):** re-measured live: 1 cross-party reply (so 1 message its sender cannot delete); enforce_message_non_sender_read_only md5 816df9b5… = 20261004001242; the FK is ON DELETE SET NULL. Migration 20261005063951 adds one branch: inside another trigger (the RI action, pg_trigger_depth() > 1), reply_to_id going to NULL, the parent already gone, and no other column changed (to_jsonb(NEW) - 'reply_to_id' = to_jsonb(OLD) - 'reply_to_id') -> allowed. A client's own clear of reply_to_id is still refused (column privilege first, the trigger behind it). Proof: src/test/pglite/replyParentDeleteCascade.pglite.mjs (prod's world, messagesWorld.mjs + 20261004001242) = 2 FAILED on prod's state ("a message may only be edited by the person who sent it"), ALL PASS applied 3x (the reply survives with reply_to_id NULL; a client still cannot clear it or edit someone else's message; the two cases that already worked still do). GUARD: src/test/replyParentDeleteCascade.test.ts (every condition of the carve-out, its position before the refusal, and the FK still SET NULL; 3 @mutate red); messagesWriteOwnership's @mutate on this function re-pointed at the new newest file (red). Needs an lh-authz-rls REVIEW-ONLY pass. done-when: sql `SELECT (prosrc ~ 'pg_trigger_depth\(\) > 1')::text FROM pg_proc WHERE proname = 'enforce_message_non_sender_read_only' AND pronamespace = 'public'::regnamespace` => true
- [~] **Q1244 MEDIUM A party can open and withdraw a dispute in a loop, and each filing pages ops and notifies people (should-fix from the lh-authz-rls re-review of Q1169, 2026-10-04; filed, not built).** rpc_open_dispute and rpc_withdraw_dispute are EXECUTE-able by authenticated (read live) and flip a job between disputed and its prior status as often as a party likes. 20261004001242 brakes the in-thread status notices (latest-notice check, 6 per participant per 10 minutes), but per the review each filing after a withdrawal also inserts a disputes row, pages Slack as critical through notify_ops_dispute_filed (called from open_dispute_as, read live; the review says it has no dedupe, not re-measured here) and notifies the other party and the admins. open_dispute_as has no cooldown: the only interval in it (read live) is the 30-day velocity count that writes a high_dispute_rate fraud flag and refuses nothing. Suggested fix (the review): in open_dispute_as, refuse a new dispute from the same opener on the same job within about 10 minutes of one they withdrew; needs an lh-money-escrow review (dispute state holds escrow). GUARD when built: a PGlite probe (open, withdraw, open again inside the window: refused with a clear code; after the window: allowed) and a pin on the cooldown predicate. In the thread itself (round-3 review, 2026-10-04): with Q1169's cap a sustained open/withdraw loop still sends up to 6 notices per participant per job per 10 minutes, about 36 pushes an hour; a cooldown on open_dispute_as also bounds that. **FIXED 2026-10-05 (security-data lane, branch lane/security-data-1005):** re-read live: rpc_open_dispute (thin wrapper, = 20260912023326) and rpc_withdraw_dispute (sets status 'withdrawn', decided_at now()) both EXECUTE authenticated; open_dispute_as (= 20261004004705, md5 706630f6…) has no cooldown. Built in rpc_open_dispute, the people's door, not in open_dispute_as, so the platform's own filings (NULL opener) are untouched: under the jobs row lock (the order every dispute RPC takes), a NEW filing by the same person on the same job within 10 minutes of a dispute THEY withdrew there raises dispute_refile_cooldown (an append to a still-open dispute and the other party's filing are not refused). helper_abort_job files through rpc_open_dispute, so a Helpr who withdrew their own dispute in the last 10 minutes waits before Cancel Job can file; both paths have copy in src/lib/lifecycleErrors.ts. Migration 20261005062746. Proof: src/test/pglite/disputeRefileCooldown.pglite.mjs = 3 FAILED on the live body, ALL PASS applied 3x. GUARD: src/test/disputeRefileCooldown.test.ts (newest definition: predicate before the filing, append path exempt, copy on both paths; 4 @mutate red). Needs an lh-money-escrow AND lh-authz-rls REVIEW-ONLY pass (dispute state holds escrow). done-when: sql `SELECT (prosrc ~ 'dispute_refile_cooldown')::text FROM pg_proc WHERE proname = 'rpc_open_dispute' AND pronamespace = 'public'::regnamespace` => true
- [ ] **Q1247 MEDIUM Recurring-visit payer is told the wrong thing, or nothing, about an on-session visit payment that cannot be booked (found by Q750 and its lh-money-escrow review, 2026-10-03; read, not reproduced in the UI).** (a) supabase/functions/stripe-webhook/handlers/settleRecurringVisitCheckout.ts refunds an unbookable on-session payment (no row, row not pending, series over, amount/currency/payer mismatch) and pages ops, but writes no notification to the payer: they see the money come back with no word from us. (b) src/pages/posts/postedJobs/RecurringVisitPayments.tsx lists every `pending` row dated after today; a row of a series that just ended stays pending until charge-recurring-visits' next daily sweep expires it (Q750 (4)), so the card offers "Pay $X" and create-payment refuses with "This series has ended". (c) The sweep (charge-recurring-visits) flips a pending row to expired BEFORE `checkout.sessions.expire`; a Checkout completed between the two is refunded in full by the webhook (the row is no longer pending), but the payer was already sent "you weren't charged". Fix (b) at the source: expire a series' pending rows in the same transaction that ends it (end_recurring_series, end_series_for_banned_account, the parent cancel) or filter the card on the parent's series_ended_on/status; fix (c) by closing the Checkout first, or wording the notice for the race. GUARD when built: an edge test that every refund branch of settleRecurringVisitCheckout writes the payer a notification, a PGlite case that ending a series leaves none of its rows pending, and an edge test that a Checkout completed mid-sweep never yields "you weren't charged".
- [ ] **Q1248 LOW A retried recurring-visit webhook posts a second "paid but not bookable — refunded" warning (lh-money-escrow review of Q750, 2026-10-03; read, not reproduced).** settleRecurringVisitCheckout counts a refund as already done only when Stripe REFUSES it (charge_already_refunded or an idempotency conflict). Within the 24h key life Stripe replays the first refund as a success, so a redelivered event (for example after the expire write threw) refunds nothing new but posts the ops warning again. Money is right; ops sees the same refund twice. Fix: compare the replayed refund's creation time, or list the intent's refunds first and skip the warning when one predates this delivery. GUARD when built: an edge test that two deliveries of one refunded payment post exactly one warning.

- [ ] **Q1319 LOW Putting a 'cancelling' claim back to 'escrow' sends 'Payment secured in escrow' and 'Job funded' again (found building Q1290, money lane, 2026-10-05; read from the live function, not seen on a device).** notify_on_payment_escrowed (prod, pg_get_functiondef read 2026-10-05) fires on NEW.payment_status = 'escrow' AND OLD distinct from 'escrow' and 'chargeback', so cancel_escrow's putCancelClaimBack (and, since Q1290, admin_refund_general's put-back when the job was 'escrow') re-announces a payment that never moved. Fix: restate the function from its live body with OLD.payment_status IS DISTINCT FROM 'cancelling' on the escrow block (as 20261003184911 did for 'chargeback'). GUARD when built: a PGlite case (escrow -> cancelling -> escrow inserts no notification; unpaid -> escrow still does), red first.
- [ ] **Q1320 MEDIUM An admin PARTIAL refund equal to the whole capture leaves the job payable, so a later payout can pay the Helpr on top (found building Q1290, money lane, 2026-10-05; code read only, NOT exercised).** admin_refund_general treats any amountCents as a partial that leaves the job running; for amountCents == the capture it re-runs the paid-out check before refunding, but takes no job claim and flips nothing, and the single-Helpr payout path (process-scheduled-payouts / release-payout) reads no refund total before transferring (grep of both for amount_refunded / payment_refunds found only the crew path's own dedupe). Fix: treat a partial equal to the capture as the full refund it is (claim, flip), or refuse it and ask for the full action. GUARD when built: a create-payment edge case, red first.

- [ ] **Q1328 MEDIUM The ops Slack digest and slack-ops-alert do not escape & < > in client-written error_logs text (lh-authz-rls review of Q1318, 2026-10-05; pre-existing, code read by the reviewer, not re-measured).** anon can already INSERT error_logs (anyone_can_insert_errors), so a client row's message can carry Slack mrkdwn (`<!channel>`, `<https://x|label>`) into send_ops_daily_digest and slack-ops-alert. Fix: escape &, <, > (Slack's three) on every client-origin field before it is posted. GUARD to add: a test that a client row with `<!channel>` reaches the digest payload escaped.
- [ ] **Q1324 LOW CSP reports share the 300/min all-guests error_logs budget with real guest crash reports (lh-authz-rls review of Q1318, 2026-10-05).** throttle_client_error_log caps guest rows at 300/min together (20/min per message+path fingerprint); csp-report rows are guest rows, so a bad CSP deploy hitting every visitor could use the budget ErrorBoundary/BootWatchdog rows need. Fix: give tags.source = 'csp' its own cap in throttle_client_error_log (migration). GUARD to add: a PGlite test that CSP rows past their cap do not consume the guest cap.
- [ ] **Q1325 LOW Nobody sees CSP violations (lh-authz-rls review of Q1318, 2026-10-05).** prod-errors-check ignores source='csp' and no admin screen shows them, so a blocked script is stored and never read. Fix: a daily count of source='csp' rows (by directive and blocked host) in the ops digest or the admin health view. GUARD to add: a test that the digest/health view includes the CSP count.
- [ ] **Q1326 LOW csp-report keeps reports for any http(s) page, not only ours (lh-authz-rls review of Q1318, 2026-10-05).** isNoise (supabase/functions/csp-report/report.ts) drops a report only when the document is not http(s), so anyone can post reports about other sites into error_logs (rate-limited per IP). Fix: keep only documentURL hosts that are ours (louisianahelpr.com, www, the Capacitor origin). GUARD to add: a case in src/test/edge/csp-report.test.ts that another host's report inserts nothing.
- [ ] **Q1327 LOW Nightly check that every pinned Action SHA is still reachable from its tag (lh-authz-rls review of Q1318, 2026-10-05; with Q1322 sha_pinning_required).** The pins (Q1323) carry a `# vX.Y.Z` comment; nothing checks the SHA is that tag's commit, so a wrong or orphaned SHA would pass the pin guard. Fix: a scheduled workflow that resolves each tag via gh api git/ref/tags and compares. GUARD: the workflow itself (red on a mismatch), with a fixture mismatch shown red once.
- [ ] **Q1329 LOW OWNER/DESIGN: does the page transition earn a 39 KB download on every route? (split from Q1299, 2026-10-05).** After the page settles, App.tsx loads PageTransition (and ScrollToTop renders its button with AnimatePresence), both framer-motion, on EVERY route, so even a guest on `/` downloads proxy.js (39 KB gz, ~90% unused by Chromium coverage, measured 2026-10-04 for Q1299). It is off the first paint (whenPageSettled), so it costs bytes, not first-paint time. Options: keep (status quo), drop the route transition on some routes, or replace both with CSS transitions (the scroll-to-top button's fade/scale is CSS-able; the route enter animation and edge-swipe back would need a rebuild). Needs the owner's call on whether the transitions stay; then a build-graph check like check-deferred-vendors.mjs for the post-settle fetch. Tier: LOW.
- [ ] **Q1330 MEDIUM A poster can still rewrite jobs.stripe_session_id on their own job (split from Q707, measured 2026-10-05 by lane/code-tooling-1005, read-only on prod).** has_column_privilege('authenticated','public.jobs','stripe_session_id','UPDATE') = true; prevent_job_field_escalation hands the poster to enforce_poster_jobs_money_lock (its poster_locked_always list, which names stripe_session_id, applies only to the offered Helpr), and enforce_poster_jobs_money_lock's locked_always / locked_when_funded lists do not name it. Where it matters: void-cancelled-payments Part A resolves the PaymentIntent from stripe_session_id when a cancelled escrow job has no stripe_payment_intent_id (index.ts ~1291), and EditJobDialog / the money lock treat a non-null session id as "funded". Measured exposure today: 0 escrow jobs without a PaymentIntent (prod, 2026-10-05), so the lookup path has nothing to act on. Fix to weigh: add stripe_session_id to the poster's locked_always (no poster path writes it; create-payment writes it as the service role), with a PGlite proof and an lh-money-escrow review. Tier: MEDIUM.
